ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u1	Dear fellow Members and fellow Members, ladies and gentlemen! Starting the 30th meeting of the National Assembly. I am informed that today's meeting cannot be attended by the following Members: Violeta Tomić, Irena Grošelj Kosnik, until 3:30 p.m. Franc Trček until 17.30, Matjaž Hanžek from 16.30 to 7.30, mag. Aleksander Kavčič, Jože Tanko from 16:00 on, Dr. László Göncz until 15:00, Jernej Vrtovac from 18:00 on, Danilo Anton Ranc to 18:00, Dr. Matthew T. Vatovec until 10 a.m. and Matjaž Han from 16:00 to 19:00. I say hello to everyone who's here! We're moving on to 15. At the end of the day, this is another defence proposal for a law amending the Public Order Act under the ordinary procedure. The draft law was tabled by Mr Andrej Chus. With regard to this proposal, the Committee on the Interior, Public Administration and Local Self-Government proposes to the National Assembly that it be decided that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration. I give the floor to the Vice-President of the Committee, Mrs Janji Slug, to further explain the proposal for a decision.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u2	Thank you very much. At its 35th meeting, on 11 May 2017, the Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government considered the draft law. Following a further explanation by the applicant, the representative of the Legal and Legislative Service, with regard to Article 1 of the draft law, said that it amends Article 71(2) of the Public Procurement Act and adds a new third paragraph. It should be taken into account that the second paragraph of this Article refers to the first and provides that in cases where this is necessary, the Government shall prescribe for individual procurement items that contracting entities shall take into account social, ethical or environmental aspects in procurement proceedings and the way in which these aspects are incorporated into the subject matter of public procurement. With regard to the amended second paragraph, she pointed out that it was not clear which other conditions the contracting authority was required to prescribe in the cases referred to in the first paragraph. The second paragraph only makes sense if the contracting authority decides on the additional social and environmental conditions, identifies them and lists them in the publication and in the procurement documents. If these conditions are mandatory, they must be laid down by law or provided for in the Government Regulation referred to in the first paragraph. However, the first paragraph of Article 71 already in force is sufficient. In addition, the proposed second amended paragraph of this Article also contains illogicality. The contracting authority must provide for social and environmental conditions, but only if they are listed in the contract notice or documentation relating to the award of the contract. This means that the contracting authority’s obligation does not apply if these conditions are not set out in the notice of procurement or documentation, since the contracting authority itself forms the contractor, so it is for the social and environmental conditions to decide in any case. The obligation to establish social and environmental conditions and criteria is thus effectively eliminated by the second conditional part of this paragraph. With regard to the amended second paragraph, the Legislative and Legal Service has also pointed out that there is still a discrepancy between the objective of the draft law and the proposed solution in the amended second paragraph. The main objective of the proposal is, inter alia, to improve the quality of food in public institutions. The amended second paragraph of Article 71 of the current law provides for the obligation of all contracting entities, i.e. all contracting entities referred to in Article 9 of the Law and not only public institutions, to make mandatory determination of the social and environmental aspects of public procurement, not just for the procurement of foodstuffs, but for each case of public procurement. With regard to the third paragraph, she pointed out that the law in force did not provide for the award of tenders and that it was only a method which had been put into practice. In view of this, the proposed provision on 50% of possible points is inconclusive, as it is not clear which points it refers to. In view of the above, he is Chairman of the Committee of Mag. Mr Verbič, under Rule 128(2) of the Rules of Procedure, proposed that the Committee decide on a proposal for a decision that the proposal for a law was not suitable for further consideration. In the debate on the draft decision, the opposition Members expressed their support for both the purpose and the reasons for the proposal for the bill, while agreeing with the opinion of the Legal and Legislative Service that it was the wrong way of putting the provisions into the current legislation. Members of the coalition warned that the proposed solutions were non-professional and superficial, which they warned both the Government and the Legal and Legislative Service. They further pointed out the legislative and legal service's warning that there is a discrepancy between the objective and the proposed solutions to the proposal and the Government's warning that the proposed solution for misplacement in the applicable law is contrary to the objective that the proposal seeks to achieve. With regard to difficulties in implementing existing provisions on the possibility of ordering locally produced food, the necessity of linking farmers has been highlighted in order to make it easier for public institutions to provide a reliable supplier who could supply the desired quantities of vegetables and other crops all the time. Following the conclusion of the discussion on the proposal for a decision, the Committee adopted the following decision: The proposal for a law amending the Public Procurement Act is not suitable for further consideration.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u3	I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Public Administration, Mag. Tanya Rich.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u4	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. President. Dear Members and Members! The Government was invited by the National Assembly to give its opinion on the proposal for a law amending the Public Procurement Act submitted to the National Assembly by Mr Andrej Chuš. It adopted its opinion on 6 April this year and in its opinion the Government opposes the proposal for a law for two reasons. The first is that the proposed regime is contrary to the objective that the proposal seeks to achieve because of the wrong placement in the law. In fact, Article 71, which is the subject of this Act, does not regulate the criteria, but lays down conditions which include social and environmental aspects. This provision also does not apply to the procurement procedure and not to the selection of tenderers, but refers to the execution phase of the contract, i.e. the contract, and therefore cannot affect the selection criteria and does not follow the objective that the applicant seeks to achieve. Moreover, this doctrine applies and binds all contracting authorities, not just public institutions. The second reason why the Government opposes the proposal for a law is that the proposal regulates the matter which is already covered by the current Public Procurement Act, namely the UNC-3, is already fully regulated in Articles 71 and 84 and the Green Public Procurement Regulation. The selection criteria are governed by Article 84 of the ZJN-3, paragraph 8, and it is already laid down in paragraph 8 that priority should be given to food products in quality schemes such as selection criteria for the procurement of foodstuffs. seasonally produced foods, in an integrated way seasonally grown foods, in an ecological manner and so on. In addition, foods produced under national food quality regulations and foodstuffs which have been produced and processed in a sustainable manner and which are guaranteed higher food quality in terms of greater freshness or lower environmental transport burdens. The current Green Procurement Regulation also regulates the share of quality elements in the selection of the operator and also sets a mandatory percentage of the proportion of organic food. At present, we are in the final stage of inter-sectoral coordination of the amendment of this Regulation and it is envisaged in this part that, in addition to the proportion of organic food, contracting authorities will also have to take into account as criteria the freshness standard for at least 5% of food, as well as the quality standard as defined for this purpose in the established schemes. In view of the above, the Government does not support the proposal for a law. Thank you very much.

For breakfast distributed under this project, schools and kindergartens offer children bread, butter, honey, apple and milk produced or processed in Slovenia. This includes a school fruit and vegetables scheme, which is a European measure of the common agricultural policy, which means the free distribution of an additional meal of fruit or vegetables to children in primary schools and institutions for the education and education of children and adolescents with special needs. It is also awareness-raising producers and food processors to report on public tenders issued by public institutions. In order to reduce bureaucratic barriers, an e-application project is under way for public procurement of food prepared by the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport. In particular, the application is intended to simplify and facilitate the ordering of food in educational institutions. The model could then be passed on to ordering food from other public contracting authorities – homes for older citizens and hospitals. Within the framework of the Rural Development Programme 2014-2020, cooperation will, more specifically, be awarded to a beneficiary, organised as a partnership between several agricultural holdings with a leading partner, which is a cooperative, independent entrepreneur, an individual or an economic company, if one or more public establishments is supplied with local products. In this way, the strategic objectives of integration, promotion of locally produced food and increased supply of public institutions with the products mentioned above are pursued. For these reasons, we will reject or support the amendment of the law in the SMC Group that the proposal is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u16	Thank you so much for saying that. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! The main objective and purpose of the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Electronic Communications (ZEKOM-1C) is to ensure the full transposition of Directive 2014/61/EU on measures to reduce costs for the deployment of electronic communications networks and services into the legal order of the Republic of Slovenia. Slovenia has so far notified the rules in force and has thereby ensured partial compliance with the Directive in question. In order to complete the transposition of the Directive, a proposal for amendments to the Law on Electronic Communications with Content should also be adopted, as proposed. The transposition of the directive into our legal order is in a hurry, as the harmonisation of the ZEKom-1C proposal has been complex and time-consuming because of many stakeholders and their many conflicting requirements. However, with these additional consultations and coordination, we wanted to ensure the most effective, quality solutions, so that there would be no difficulties in its implementation in practice. In addition to the content related to the comprehensive transposition of the Directive, the law also regulates certain improvements in parts where it has in practice proved necessary. The proposal for a law by content, as it is said, is largely based on the requirements of the Directive, this is a directive of minimum harmonisation which still leaves countries with a considerable degree of flexibility to regulate certain specifications, national specifications. Slovenia has already introduced certain advanced solutions before this Directive and the proposal for a law in certain places exceeds the minimum requirements of the Directive. ZEKom-1C to the fullest extent intervenes in the chapter on the construction of networks and related infrastructure and in the section on the provision of competition. The solutions proposed are aimed at promoting the construction of public communications networks, the development of services for end-users, the reduction of construction costs and the promotion of the common use of existing infrastructure, not only of electronic communications infrastructure, but also of other economic public infrastructures. Some other essential solutions that the law also has. With regard to the determination of the number of points for the calculation of the payment paid by the operators by AKOS, i.e. the Agency for Communications Networks and Services of the Republic of Slovenia, for its operation, we have followed the aspect of the proportional burden in the search for a solution, so that only half of the annual revenue from these services is taken into account by operators on a reasoned proposal for revenue from the public communications services of international transport, which is entirely originating and ending outside Slovenia. In the radio spectrum section, disagreements in the interpretation of the provisions of the ZEKom-1 and the law governing the media have led to the elimination of some inconsistencies in the delimitation of powers between the AKOS and the Broadcasting Council in the process of assigning radio frequencies for analogue broadcasting. For the benefit of end-users, in the interests of clarity, the provisions on contracting contracts are complemented and it requires operators to provide opportunities for monitoring and control of the use of data services. The powers of the Council of the Agency shall also be increased, which shall adopt the work programme and the financial plan and monitor their implementation. The requirements for candidates for the Director and members of the Board of the Agency to ensure the transparency of their interests are also increasing. We propose that the bill should be supported by a vote, thus allowing its further consideration and thus ensuring that the law is adopted as quickly as possible. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u17	We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. The word is given by the New Slovenia Group of Christian Democrats, Mrs Iva Dimic.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u18	Dear President, distinguished Secretary of State, Members and Members of Parliament! The proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Electronic Communications brings Directive 2014/61/EU to the Slovenian legal order. In view of the current requirements for the construction or reconstruction of multi-family and business buildings, and in accordance with the requirements of Directive 2014/61/EU, the proposal introduces an adjustment to the arrangements according to which the communication infrastructure in the joint parts of the building must be placed in the physical infrastructure of the building, i.e. in empty installation channels. It is precisely because of the possibility of later connecting end users to the high-speed network in New Slovenia that we support these adjustments. In addition, in order to provide information as well as to make sense, we see the possibility that a network operator, when basic information on existing physical infrastructure is not available in a pooled cadastra, will request this information from any infrastructure operator in the area concerned. The proposal provides that the Agency must explain its decision on the public call for radio frequencies accordingly, which is welcome for ensuring transparency. The proposal also allows users, within the framework of their domestic consumption, to be familiar with the use of data services or to set a financial or quantitative limit which they cannot exceed without specific authorisation. The possibility of setting such a limit, in our view, primarily for users who are slightly less skilled in the use of data transmission, increases online security. In New Slovenia we welcome the obligation to transfer emergency calls from vehicles to the authorities they deal with. This is a European Union project that implements a mechanism that automatically informs Call Centre 112 in case of an accident and thus saves a lot of valuable time to rescue. In accordance with this proposal, the authorities will have to upgrade their emergency call detection equipment and its transmission to the authority handling the call. It's about setting up a system to save lives. The proposal for this Act also requires the Agency for Communications Networks and Services to publish on its websites a link to information on the conditions and procedures for the authorisation of construction works needed to establish elements of high-speed electronic communications networks. The Agency’s concern is also, for example, to provide stakeholders with an opportunity to learn about relevant information. Since the proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Electronic Communications makes some meaningful changes, in the New Slovenia Group we will vote in favour of the conclusion that the proposal is suitable for further consideration.

So far, the powers between the Broadcasting Council and the Agency for Communications Networks and Services have overlapped or been unclear. It is further improving the security of networks and their operation in emergency situations, as cyber security, in the light of a recent attack at European Union level, has become a very important area. That is why we decided in the SMC Group that we would support the proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Electronic Communications.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u23	The text is given by the Group of the Democratic Party of the Pensioners of Slovenia, for which Mr Peter Wilfan has spoken.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u24	Dear President, colleagues and colleagues, the rest are present! We have before us a proposal for a law that we will unanimously support in the Desus Group at first reading. Its purpose is to strengthen the functioning of the internal market and to promote coverage through the broadband network, which is a condition for the development of the Digital Single Market. With the adoption of the law, we will bring into the Slovenian legal order a directive introducing measures to reduce the costs of building high-speed electronic communications networks. We expect positive impacts on the prevalence of the use of high-speed internet, whose digital infrastructure supports a modern and innovative economy. The proposal provides that only half of the annual revenue from public communications services of international transport, which originates in full and ends outside Slovenia, shall be taken into account by operators in determining the number of payment points paid to the Agency. In the area of construction, it is stipulated that the communication infrastructure in the joint parts of the building must be placed in the building physical infrastructure, i.e. in the empty installation channels, due to the possibility of further integration of end-users into the high-speed network. At the same time, categories of buildings for which this obligation does not apply shall be determined. The Agency for Communications Networks and Services undertakes to draw up at least a three-year radio spectrum management strategy and to publish it on its websites after obtaining the Government's consent. Since cyber security at EU level has become a very important area at global level, broader action is also envisaged at national level and the proposal for a law also allows future development in this area, irrespective of the body responsible for cyber security. In the Desus Group we welcome the purpose of the bill to provide services for public mobile information and alarm systems in the event of major natural and other disasters. A more important solution is the solution that requires operators to upgrade their emergency call detection equipment and to transmit it to the body that is dealing with it. The bill will therefore, in addition to the transposition of the directive into the Slovenian legal order, improve the legal regime in force and we will therefore vote in the Desus Group for further consideration.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u25	The word is given by the Group of Social Democrats, a magi for her. Bojana Mursich.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u26	Thank you very much, President, again for your word. Today, we have a comprehensive proposal for a law, both in terms of articles and in terms of scope, and from a substantive point of view, it is a very difficult law. The area covered by electronic communications is met daily mainly by the use of the internet, telephone, television receivers, but it is much more complex and important than anyone at first sight thinks. But it takes a lot of knowledge and integration to get through material that's so important and so technical. Why? Electronic communication infrastructure is very important for all sectors of the country, and professional and interested public were invited to prepare the most possible regulation of life. It is clear from the material provided that a large number of stakeholders were involved in the preparation of this proposal. Or this also means improving the law and it will be seen in the next legislative procedure. The main objective of this novelty is to introduce measures by means of the transposition of Directive 2014/61/EU that will help to reduce the costs of the construction of high-speed electronic communications networks. Reducing costs and improving the efficiency of the construction of high-speed electronic communications is essential for the spread of high-speed internet, whose digital infrastructure supports virtually all sectors of the modern and innovative economy. The time limit for transposition of this Directive has expired already in January 2016 and is therefore in a hurry to adopt the law. However, the European Commission's threat to the Republic of Slovenia because of the non-transferability of the Directive must not be the reason for the swift and reckless amendment of those provisions of the Electronic Communications Act, which are not bound by the Directive, and between the professional and interested public it brings more disagreements as a solution. Electronic communications are part of our daily lives today, and it is therefore very important how and how this spine will be protected from hacker intrusions into the main infrastructure systems of the country. At the same time, it is also necessary to provide for the supervision and processing of personal data and the protection of the privacy of electronic communications. In the radio spectrum section, disagreements in the interpretation of the provisions of the ZEKom-1 and the law governing the media and, in the opinion of the applicant, are eliminated with regard to the delimitation of powers between the Agency and the Broadcasting Council in the process of assigning radio frequencies for analogue broadcasting. The powers of the Board of the Agency shall be enhanced to adopt the Agency’s programme and financial plan and to monitor their implementation. The Board of the Agency will no longer have the power, as proposed, to propose the appointment of the Director of the Agency, as the requirements for candidates for the Director and members of the Board of the Agency for ensuring the transparency of their interests are increasing. In order to avoid potential conflicts of interest, the irreconcilability of the Director’s and his deputies’ functions through their other activities extends to prohibited business, to business cooperation in relation to an activity under the responsibility of the Agency. I have only mentioned some amendments and additions to this important regulation. However, at this point, I might also take the proposaler’s conclusion on the Constitutional Court’s decision of 3. Seven. This Decision shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The Constitutional Court has repealed the entire chapter of the law relating to data storage. The Constitutional Court also decided that all information obtained under Article 163 of the Act must be destroyed immediately after the decision was published in the Official Journal. In addition, it should be stressed that the European Union court declared the Data Storage Directive, which Slovenia has transposed into the national legal order, invalid. We are confident that the debate on the appropriateness of individual solutions will only develop in the course of the legislative process, due to comments from both the professional and the interested public, but we believe that the proposal for a law is suitable for further consideration, so we will support it. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u27	The word is given by the Group of Members of the United Left, for her Luka Moon.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u28	Dear Mr. President! Dear Members and Members! The area of electronic communications is one of the key areas for the future of Slovenia, as well as for the development of the world at all. In the areas of new technologies, we can talk about two conflicting logics. One is the one who closes and uses these technologies primarily to control and pursue commercial interests, and the other is the one that opens up, with new technologies, to create new opportunities for broad participation and to promote public interest. It is in this light that we in the United Left are assessing legislative proposals from the fields of electronic communications. As far as regulation and the legislative framework are concerned, the situation in Slovenia is not exactly pink. We have some examples of arrangements that are brighter exceptions, where we are better off than other European countries, because our solutions are among the more advanced. This is the case with the current regime on internet neutrality, but I will talk more about this further, but unfortunately the general electronic communication in Slovenia is still inadequately regulated. For years we have been able to hear calls for the necessity of a new Law on Electronic Communications, so-called ZEKom-2. We have heard promises from the ministries on numerous occasions that this will happen. We already have them today, or we will, I'm sure of that. There are currently two key problems in the area of electronic communications regulation. The first is inadequate consumer protection or better said inadequate protection of public interest, while the second is a wrong and ineffective control and management framework. Here I am referring to the triangle of the Akos, the Electronic Communications Council and the Broadcasting Council. When considering this new amendment to the Law on Electronic Communications, we must count on the fact that all the work so far and all the words so far have been confirmed. Minister Koprivnikar advocates that trend, which wants to use information technology in particular for the pursuit of commercial interests, for increasing subordination of public sector employees and supervision of citizens. The Ministry of Education in this field is not better or more successful. The transposition of the directive, which is the central part of the novel under consideration, should be carried out by the end of 2015, which means we are two years behind schedule. With regard to the novel, I would like to point out four points. The first is internet neutrality. In the previous draft, which was sent to public hearing a while ago, the Ministry of Public Administration wanted to turn online neutrality into the worst in accordance with a regulation adopted by the European Parliament and was lobbyed by the largest multinationals in the field of telecommunications. The European Parliament adopted a regulation and the regulation contained a number of dedicated legal loopholes that would allow large telecommunications companies to unfairly discriminate against Internet traffic, thereby jeopardising the openness, neutrality and democratic nature of the media. Fortunately, the Association of European Supervisors of BEREC then issued guidelines for the functioning of national regulators, which took account of their commitment to the principle of internet neutrality. However, every word of Article 65 of the novel should be carefully considered, as the provision is still questionable in terms of the neutrality of the Internet. The second is the change with regard to AKOS. In the light of past experience, it can be found that the change in the functioning of AKOS unfortunately makes the situation worse but not improves. During the time of the Government of Mir Cerar, it has proved that AKOS is not independent of every political or party interest and is even less independent of commercial interests. Attention should be drawn to the problem of so-called rotating doors; those who are supposed to supervise come from or depart from positions in controlled companies. The stories about profit practices of the highest official, sublime business cooperation and so on are also resounding. [[…]] At the same time, the Council for Electronic Communications has become an extension of the policy, while at the same time the issue of its competence or placement. In other words, the Council has no role to play. The law does not solve this; it even complicates the situation with some articles, which will lead to even worse functioning and even more imprudent. The actual consequences of this novel will be that there will be an even greater political subordination of the AKOS. The third issue to be mentioned in the specific novelty is the transposition of a directive aimed at promoting comprehensive coverage through broadband. At the abstract level, of course, this is an urgent and very positive objective that we welcome in the United Left, but it is a directive of minimum harmonisation, which gives Member States more flexibility in regulating this area widely in national legislation. However, the arrangement brought by the novel to the Zekem goes into great detail. Why? For the novel is not written neutrally, but according to the measure of known actors. The law is written on the skin of the largest operators, and it also assumes certain powers for local communities. In the case of broadband coverage, the private interests of telecommunications capital, rather than the public's interests, are being promoted. The fourth point is consumer protection. The provisions of the novel do not eliminate critical points in consumer rights, and we know, for example, from the practices of the biggest Slovenian operators such as Simobil, now A1, and Telekom, that in the field of electronic communications there is a great deal for consumers of harmful practices, not to say even fraud and abuse. We would need clear and decisive solutions, but only beauty corrections are proposed and in a way that leaves a lot of open space for harmful practices. Finally, the legislative process and the draft show that the Ministry, led by Boris Koprivnik, did not take into account the comments of the interested and professional public, taking into account to a greater extent the interests of private companies and their lobbyists. In the United Left of the New Law on Electronic Communications, we cannot therefore support and do not understand it as suitable for further consideration. Not only does it prolong the inappropriate state, it consciously goes in the direction of the wrong logic that I mentioned at the outset. However, as the coalition appears to confirm the amendment, in the United Left we will propose appropriate amendments to the law, which will put public interest and consumer protection first in the fields of electronic communications.

In view of the differences in the interpretation of the provisions of the ZEKs, the new law now stipulates that "... for the allocation of radio frequencies for analogue broadcasting, the distinction between the conditions and criteria relating to the evaluation of programme content and other conditions and criteria shall be abolished." The SRD will no longer define the conditions and criteria, so it cannot block the issuance of frequencies anymore but merely gives its written opinion in advance, and the conditions and criteria will be determined by the radio-spectrum management agency and is also responsible for its decisions. However, the Ministry of Culture, which has the competence to manage the development tasks in the media field, will, following the proposal for this legislative amendment, give its consent to the conditions and criteria to be laid down by the Agency. Thus, this blockade, which has been in the past and has also been pointed out at the Committee on Culture meeting, is lifted and what Mrs Anja Bah Žibert says is not true. As regards the issue of the judgment of the Constitutional Court, as discussed by Mr Grims, it should be noted that the proposal of this Act will amend Article 151 to read and quote: “Traffic data which do not relate to subscribers and users, and which has been processed and stored by the operator, must be deleted or amended in such a way that they cannot be linked to a particular or identifiable person immediately” – I emphasise that “when it is no longer necessary for the transmission of communications, except in cases where a longer period of storage has been established under this law with regard to the monitoring and control of data services” – it is said that the user wishes to know how much he has used — “the view of the call in force, as regards internal procedures, the security and the indelible registration of the provision of traffic and location data in cases of life and body protection, as regards the tracing of malicious or the competent parties to the communication referred to in Article 160 of the Rules of Procedure. It is said that this case is regulated in accordance with a decision of the Constitutional Court and it is difficult to claim at this point that the proposal does not make any progress. After my assessment, it's quite concrete progress. There has been a lot of talk about what the former Director has done wrong, and in this regard, the law has also systematically introduced significant improvements, such as "candidates for the Director and a member of the Board of the Agency are obliged to make a statement to reveal their links and interests which could be affected by the decisions of the Agency". Further, “in order to prevent potential conflicts of interest, the irreconcilability of the functions of the Director and his deputies with their other activities shall be extended to the prohibition of business cooperation with natural or legal persons engaged in activities in the field under the responsibility of the Agency. Membership in the administrative and supervisory bodies of legal persons engaged in business with natural or legal persons carrying out activities in the field under the responsibility of the Agency and holding the ownership shares of such legal persons.” So, the director should not have ownership in companies of some interest, and it may be suspected here that he has been contaminated, should not sit in the administrative and supervisory bodies of these persons, who might have a certain interest, which would conflict with his function, so that he should not cooperate with these natural and legal persons, and here the law seems to me that this potential spectrum of abuse or potential abuse of any director who would lead the agency is greatly expanded. Next, about the world. The Council of the Agency is given greater powers and, of course, this improved supervision. It monitors the implementation of the work programme and the financial plan, following a new one. It is therefore important to stress here that the Council of the Agency no longer merely issues opinions on the work programme and the financial plan, but accepts it. We have further heard that the Agency will also have to present at least a three-year regulatory strategy. All of these are concrete improvements that reduce the possibility of potential abuses and bad practices that have occurred in the past, so that the law will be declared bad at first, to appeal to coalition now, to dramatize, to call for understanding and so on, I think – exaggeration. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u55	For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Infrastructure mag. I'm putting pressure on it. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u56	Thank you, Vice President. Dear Members and honourable Members! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia does not support the proposal for a law on amendments to the Road Traffic Rules Act submitted by a group of Members and Members with the first signed Jozet Tank to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia. The proposed law proposes that the blue light and sound signal be used by firefighters of the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh and eighth categories who perform fire service in firefighting units, using their personal vehicles on their way to the fire department when called for intervention. Candidates for the use of the blue lamp and siren would be appointed by the Commander of the fire liaison of the municipality on the basis of the municipal alarm plan. The latter would also issue, within 8 days, a written authorisation for the use of the blue lamp and siren, after the course has been completed. This would allow the use of a blue light with a siren to all fire extinguishers of all units, in order to respond as quickly, safely and effectively as possible to the call. The Act in force on road traffic rules in Article 101, paragraph 1, provides that vehicles with the advantage and accompanying vehicles shall be motor vehicles on which special light and sound signals, signs, are used to perform certain emergency tasks. Vehicles with an advantage to which it is permitted to install devices for the provision of special light and audible signs indicating vehicles with an advantage and in some cases the use of special light and audible signs on vehicles with an advantage shall be permitted, and shall be subject to the provisions of the fourth indent of Article 11(1) of the Law, in agreement with the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, in agreement with the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. Therefore, the proposer of the Complementary Act on Road Traffic Rules wants certain vehicles to be designated by law with the advantage of installing devices for the provision of special lighting and sound signs indicating vehicles with an advantage. So the personal vehicles of firefighters first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh and eighth categories used by firefighters on their way to the unit when called for intervention. Other vehicles with an advantage to which it is permitted to install devices for the provision of special lighting and sound signs indicating vehicles with an advantage shall remain defined in a sub-legislative regulation issued by the Minister responsible for Transport in agreement with the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. The implementation of the proposed regulation, a two-track regime, would constitute a breach of the principle of certainty in the drafting of regulations, as the principle of certainty requires the preparation of generally understandable and clear rules, which ensures legal certainty, trust in law and equality before law and prevents the different interpretation or application of rules in practice. In addition to that proposal, the law does not address the relevant issues on which the proposed regulation affects; for example, the determination of the reasons and conditions for the withdrawal of the authorisation, the composition of the course for users of the blue lamp, the coverage of the costs of carrying out the individual vehicle authorisation procedure and the upgrading of the vehicle, liability for damage in the event of a traffic accident, the determination of the financial consequences of the bill. In view of the number of possible users, we are talking about firefighters of the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh and eighth categories who perform both voluntary and professional fire-fighting services in fire-fighting units, creating significantly more opportunities for abuse of vehicles with an advantage and thus having a negative impact on the safety of all road users. We must be aware that we have around 117 000 firefighters in Slovenia in this honour activity, which would, in fact, have granted this right to their personal vehicles, somehow amount to up to 10% of Slovenia's rolling stock has an advantage in transport, which is extremely large. For all the reasons mentioned above, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia does not support the Proposal for a Law on Supplements to the Road Traffic Rules Act submitted by a group of Members and Members to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia with the first-signed Joze Tank and considers that it is not appropriate for further consideration. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u58	Dear President, distinguished representatives of the Government, honourable Members and Members! The proposal for a law on amendments to the Road Traffic Rules Act proposes a solution allowing the use of a blue light and a sound signal for voluntary firefighters or voluntary firefighters using their personal vehicles on their way to the unit when called for intervention. In accordance with the current regulation, however, devices for giving specific lighting and sound signals indicating vehicles with an advantage may only be used in the vehicle rules with the advantage of specified vehicles, including fire extinguishers. The opinion on whether the above warning signals should also be made available to voluntary firefighters in passenger vehicles is shared and, from the point of view of the expert point of view, it presupposes that such a measure is not appropriate or even more that it may be harmful in terms of safety. It is important to be aware that voluntary firefighting is very developed in Slovenia compared to other countries. According to the Firefighters Association of Slovenia, there are about 132 000 members of voluntary fire companies. Of this, there are some 60,000 active firefighters and 133 000 of all members of voluntary firefighters. Compared to the number of professional firefighters who are around 860 and mentioned numbers, it can be imagined that the solution proposed to enable the use of blue light and sound signal also for voluntary firefighters or voluntary firefighters using their personal vehicles on their way to the unit would be a significant increase in the number of vehicles with an advantage. The latter may have a negative impact on the safety of road users. In addition, it is not negligible that as many as a quarter of voluntary firefighters under 19 years of age would be given the power to drive under special, substantially more risky conditions when proposed amendments are made as young drivers. In addition, the proposal for a law is also technically flawed as it opens up a legal void with regard to the rules and conditions for the authorisation and in the proposal for additional education for the use of the intervention light in passenger vehicles. The contents which are regulated in the Regulation after the applicable road safety regime are complemented and the proposed amendment is therefore not in accordance with the applicable regulation. A number of outstanding questions are also raised regarding the proposed solution, such as how long is it granted to the fire extinguisher to use this intervention light, when can it be withdrawn, who can take the course, who will cover the vehicle upgrade costs, how will the insurance of these vehicles, which are subject to greater risk and so on. Given that the proposed solution covers a large number of firefighters, this would significantly increase the number of vehicles with an advantage, which would have a negative impact on the safety of road users. For these reasons, in particular because of the proposed solution to reduce road safety, we do not consider that it is appropriate for further consideration in the Contingent Group of the Modern Centre for this proposal.

The purpose of the law is to adapt the use of the blue light and the sound signal for voluntary firefighters or voluntary fire brigades using their personal vehicles on their way to the unit and when called for intervention. This would allow the use of the blue lamp to all firefighters of all units, when called for intervention, in order to respond as quickly, safely and effectively as possible to the call, provided that they have the written authority of the commander of the fire liaison of the municipality and on the basis of the municipal alarm plan and the course completed. The candidate should also take a course in the use of the blue light or blue module on passenger vehicles. After the course, the candidate would receive a marked light and sound device and a document or permit to use the code with his name ID. The module is not attached to the vehicle, it's connected to the person. The misuse of the use of the blue module would be subject to sanctions under the current regime. Here we would like to point out when the number of firefighters was mentioned – about 133 thousand, even when intervention occurs, you do not know in advance who will go, probably not all of these 133 thousand, but probably the potential of firefighters who would be entitled to use this status or vehicle with advantage. This means that somehow every eighth vehicle in Slovenia would have a status of advantage, which is extremely large, and we believe that in terms of security this could compromise security because it would probably cause chaos. On the other hand, when we get used to some measure that should warn us that something is different, that there is something special in traffic, and these are light and sound signals, if such vehicles equipped with these signals are so much more, we become absolutely less careful about it. I would also like to point out that most members of voluntary fire companies live in the immediate vicinity or very close to the firehouse or fire department, which would mean the use of vehicles with the advantage of shorter relationships. There is usually no traffic or congestion on local roads to prevent firefighters from coming from home to a firehouse or fire department. In addition, short driving in accordance with the prescribed maximum speed would not significantly prolong the response time of firefighters. Ordering vehicles with an advantage in the Republic of Croatia – of course we went to look at the legal order elsewhere – does not deviate from the regulation in Slovenia. Devices for the provision of special lighting and sound signs indicating vehicles with an advantage shall only be allowed to be installed and used on emergency medical vehicles, ambulances and fire-fighting vehicles, on civil protection vehicles, police vehicles and military police vehicles; not on private-owned fire extinguishers. The proposed solution, in our view, may also be initiated by other comparable services or potential users who must immediately come to work or other activities related to life-saving, health and property on duty at home or other similar work institutes. Here I have in mind doctors, criminals, soldiers, other security and rescue forces, members of a special police unit, officers standing by. Taking into account the proposed solutions and any new proposals, this would mean that this increase in vehicles with the advantage would not only be so great that it would be every eighth vehicle as proposed, but even more. Here I might also point out that vehicles with the advantage of the Motor Vehicle Act are considered to be processed vehicles, even if it is a special warning lamp, which is why Article 19 of the Motor Vehicle Act provides for the individual approval of such a vehicle. In accordance with Article 50, such vehicles shall also have a purpose on their marketing authorisation. Such vehicles shall then also be subject to a technical inspection every 6 months, as has already been said. There's one more thing I'd like to draw your attention to. This status gets the vehicle, this is a vehicle with an advantage and such a vehicle is not limited only to a voluntary firefighter. This vehicle is a private vehicle. A private car that can actually be driven by anyone who is allowed by the owner of this vehicle to do so. How he will use such a vehicle is a matter for the driver to take over such a vehicle. He does not necessarily use it for the purpose or the way that was provided for in the good spirit of the law. Here I might point out just one more thing in the end, so I won't be too long. It's an assault on the vehicle and everything I said before. As far as the hierarchy of laws is concerned, I would point out that the current users who can use or are entitled to vehicles with the advantage defined in the Regulation are users. This amendment of the law, however, provides that this is already laid down in the law only for new users. Only for these new proposed users, these are voluntary firefighters and passenger vehicles, and this is stipulated in the law. We would have different arrangements for several categories of users. I would conclude that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia does not support, in view of the above, the Proposal for a Law on Complements of the Road Traffic Law and considers that it is not appropriate for further consideration.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u120	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! On 19 October 2016, the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia adopted a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Motor Vehicles and a bill amending and supplementing the Law on Road Traffic Rules. The objective of the two novelties was, in the view of the applicant, to ensure higher safety standards for the use of bicycles with auxiliary engines whose maximum design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour and to lay the appropriate legal basis for regulating the scope of these vehicles. Let me start by saying that bicycles may also include vehicles whose design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. This category includes mopeds, which thus reach higher final speeds, have built-in blocks that are relatively easy to remove. The law entered into force on 1 May 2017 and we can say that the law adopted in practice has put bicycle owners with a motor ahead of almost insurmountable problems. Many for their bikes with an engine they bought 10, 20, 30, 40 and more years ago have no ownership certificate and other documentation. Therefore, the ownership of the vehicle will have to, and they have to, prove at that time in a finding procedure which is complex and long-lasting. They had to find witnesses, some had already died, some 10 years ago, and they confirmed that the moped's owners were over 10 years old, and so on. This isn't where these things stopped either. It is a specific case where moped owners cannot prove ownership because they were not older than 18 years at the time of engine production, i.e. full-year. They're bordering on, we can say, even a comedy of absurdity. We have some mopeds or construction vehicles up to 25, which are a few 10 years old, and are looking for witnesses who are almost 100 years old. It's absurd matters, it's an absurd law that has nothing to do with road safety. As already stated in 2016, respected colleagues and colleagues, 25 kilometers per hour is the speed achieved by a healthy man in progress, 25 kilometres per hour is the speed achieved by each animal, from dog to horse, 25 kilometers per hour exceeds each skier, 25 kilometers per hour is the speed that has left these mopeds unregistered for decades. We're talking about wheels with an auxiliary engine. Anomalies in the law are also in different areas, even in a cost-effective sense. Mopeds have to perform a technical inspection, those mopeds after 2004 have to complete a complete approval costing about €90. Vehicles over 8 years of age must undergo a technical inspection each year. There are also plates for these mopeds that are special. The main solutions to the bill are to amend the Motor Vehicle Act or to abolish the obligation to register bicycles with auxiliary motors or mopeds whose maximum design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. This also removes insurmountable administrative obstacles and complications that have been brought before the owners by a law on mandatory registration of mopeds where the permanent rated power of the engine does not exceed 4 kilowatts and structurally defined speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. The abolition of this law will have no financial implications. The texts of the articles to be repealed are mainly bound by Article 31 — Conditions for registration; they are bound by Article 50 — Time-limits for carrying out technical checks; and, in particular, they are bound by Article 3 — Transitional and final provisions to abolish what the current law has imposed, so that after May 1, 2017 the wheels must be supported by an auxiliary engine whose permanent rated power of the engine does not exceed 4 kilowatts and a structurally defined speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. The proposal for this novel, which is following a regular procedure in the first reading, suggests that this law, which can be said to be an insult to any logic, for every healthy farm of intelligence, should be abolished because it has nothing to do with road safety. I will also explain and discuss all other matters in practice that have arisen with the implementation of this law in the coming minutes and hours. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u121	Thank you very much. For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Infrastructure mag. I'm putting pressure on it. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u122	Thank you, Chairman. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! The proposal for a law amending the Law on Motor Vehicles, as proposed by the Group of Members and Members with the first-signed Jozet Tank to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia in April 2017, is not supported by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia opposes changes in the law, as the proposed articles interfere with the easier identification of ownership for all mopeds and repeals the provision setting the time limit for technical inspection for all vehicles. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia opposes that change in the easier identification of ownership for all mopeds, as it is necessary to facilitate registration of mopeds. It also opposes the abolition of a provision specifying the technical inspection of the vehicle before its registration in the Republic of Slovenia if the vehicle is older than the time limit for the first technical inspection of the vehicle of the same category. This provision shall apply to all vehicles. Those measures are not solely related to mopeds where the permanent rated power of the propulsion engine does not exceed 4 kilowatts and the structural specified speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour, and are necessary for the smooth functioning of the vehicle registration system and technical checks to complement road safety measures for all its participants. According to the material of the Proposal for a Law amending the Motor Vehicle Act, this Act should abolish the obligation to register mopeds where the permanent rated power of the engine does not exceed 4 kilowatts and the design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. However, it follows from the normative part that only the transitional period for the start of the mandatory registration of those mopeds shall be abolished. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia points out that the transitional provision is consumed and is not allowed to be affected. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia also opposes the intention to abolish the obligation to register these mopeds. The registration of mopeds in which the permanent rated power of the engine does not exceed 4 kilowatts and the design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour has been introduced with a view to ensuring higher safety standards for all road users, the identification of the owners of these mopeds and the insurance of potential injuries in traffic accidents with the mopeds concerned. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u134	Mr President, thank you for your floor. Colleagues, colleagues! The proposal for a law amending the Motor Vehicle Act, according to the applicant, contains only one supplement, namely the abolition of the obligation to register bicycles with an auxiliary engine or so-called mopeds whose maximum design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. The applicant assesses that the mandatory registration of these vehicles has led to insurmountable administrative obstacles and complications imposed on owners by the legal obligation to register mopeds. It should be recalled that last year, on the initiative and with the signature of all coalition members, SMC, Desus and SD, two novelties were tabled and then adopted, namely the Motor Vehicles Act and the Road Traffic Rules Act, with a very clear objective, this is to ensure higher cycle safety standards with engines or so-called mopeds and consequently all other road users. In the field, they faced an irregular state that did not allow transparency over the number of these mopeds. Neither the traceability of mopeds and drivers nor their ownership was ensured. These so-called twenty-fives drive largely and were also driven by young drivers at the time, the use of protective helmets was not mandatory, mopeds were therefore not required to be registered and therefore virtually impossible to track in the event of offences and accidents. Numerous media, police, and the professional public have pointed out that in practice there is a significant number of infringements, including those relating to the processing of these engines by users with a view to increasing speed in order to remove built-in blockades, causing them to exceed speeds of over 50 kilometres per hour. Today, the law entered into force and the registration of mopeds was, as it was said, introduced to ensure higher safety standards for all road users, and the proposed law would actually come back to the period before the two laws were adopted. As mopeds have so far not been defined as an exception to the requirement for proof of origin and ownership of the vehicle and have not been part of the mandatory vehicle registration system, some implementing issues have actually arisen in the field of proof of ownership. These evidentiary problems were mainly related to the fact that the official conducting the registration procedure had a discretionary right to decide which evidence was necessary to prove ownership. As new procedures have been introduced, there have been various practices in practice and, according to the Ministry, this has been abolished. In fact, the Ministry of Infrastructure has aligned the procedure with instructions which have been transmitted to all those carrying out these registration procedures. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we estimate that the effects of the law are so far positive regardless of the administrative problems that occurred at the start of the registrations of these vehicles, and we also consider that this law that we have today on the table is a kind of adjustment. It is worth noting that it was so late that, according to the Ministry of Infrastructure, we already have over 7,000 such mopeds that are registered and that returning to a previous situation would in fact constitute one property damage that could have been imposed by all those drivers who would have been found to have made registrations that would no longer be necessary under this bill. Given that it was also from the perspective of other parliamentary groups, even at the time of the adoption of previous laws, it is more than clear that we are all united in that these mopeds need a certain identification that they need insurance. I believe that the debate on the need for this regulation is, in fact, merely some political prestige and that it is necessary to leave this place in place to be realized in practice and that all those involved in transport ultimately enjoy the security that this law provides for them. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u179	The draft law was treated by the Health Committee as a parent working body. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to the Vice-President, Mr Branko Zorman. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u180	Dear colleagues, fellow Secretary of State! At its 23rd meeting, on 10 May 2017, the Health Committee considered the draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Limitation of the Consumption of Alcohol, submitted by a group of Members and Members to the National Assembly for consideration and adoption following a reduced procedure, with the first signed by Janie Möderndorfer. No amendments were tabled within the time limit set. Mr Jani Möderndorfer gave the opening supplementary explanation. He pointed out that the Alcohol Restriction Act was adopted in 2003, on the basis of which a number of measures and ways of limiting alcohol were implemented. The amendment and amendment of the law find that some positive effects have certainly been brought about by the law, but it has been shown that all prohibitions and restrictions have not achieved the desired objective. The main objective of the bill is to increase the financing of sports organisations from their own resources and to regulate the limited possibility of watering drinks in order to prevent the illegal introduction or over-consuming of alcoholic beverages at public events. The novelty of the bill is the restriction of organisers with permission, which brings greater responsibility for the supply of drinks in accordance with the law, since in the event of non-compliance with the legislation they may lose their license. To organisers of most public sports events, additional revenues represent an important source of income for the performance of activities. The opinion of the Legal and Legislative Service was presented by Mrs Mira Palhartinger. She stressed that the solution in which the supply and sale of alcoholic beverages at public events could only be allowed to the organisers of such events and only on the basis of authorisation, in terms of the freedom of the economic initiative in relation to the principle of equality, could be restrictive. The proposed arrangements should therefore be thoroughly assessed in the light of the urgency, appropriateness and proportionality of the measure, in view of the objective it pursues. While proposals for amendments to the Committee address some shortcomings, they do not fully address them. The opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia was presented by the State Secretary of the Ministry of Health, Mrs Jožica Maučec Zakotnik. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia does not support that proposal. The opinion of the Commission for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons of the State Council was presented by mag. Peter Pozun. He stressed that the Commission does not support the proposed amendment of the law and considers it inappropriate to interfere with the legal measure which has so far proved to be good. In addition, representatives of the Health Council, the Medical Chambers of Slovenia and some NGOs presented their views on the draft law. In a broad and controversial debate, different views and opinions were expressed as to the proposed amendments to the law, as well as data on the use and consumption of alcohol between Slovenians and comparative data with other countries. The bill was therefore opposed by representatives of professional medical organisations and representatives of NGOs. Proponents of the bill pointed out that sports clubs in Slovenia have a poor financial position due to the consequences of the financial and economic crisis. This change would allow for additional income generated by sports clubs rather than guest bars nearby. The Committee adopted its amendments to Articles 1 and 2 of the bill. In accordance with Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on the articles of the bill and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, is prepared on the basis of the first subparagraph of Article 133 of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended draft law is an integral part of this report. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u181	For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Health, Mrs Jožici Maučec Zakotnik. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u182	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House, ladies and gentlemen of the House! We have before us a proposal for amendments to the Law on the Limitation of Alcohol Consumption, which after 15 years allows the sale of alcohol again at sports events. This would increase the availability of alcohol in a country which has no problems with the harmful consequences of its use. Increasing the financial resources of sports organisations by selling alcohol is contrary to the basic objective of the current law, which is to limit the consumption of alcohol and to prevent the harmful consequences of its use. Equalisation of the treatment of the pouring of alcoholic beverages at all public events, which will increase the accessibility and promotion of alcoholic beverages, does not follow the objectives of the legislation in force. The bill is contrary to international strategic documents and scientific evidence, which include, among the most effective measures to mitigate the harm, the right limitation of access to alcohol, the increase in the price of alcoholic beverages, the restriction of advertising and the early recognition and treatment of those who are at risk of drinking or who are dependent on alcohol. The proposed law does not follow. On the contrary. With the new arrangement, we increase the number of sales places and opportunities for advertising alcoholic beverages. This will contribute to an increase in the risk and harmful use of alcohol, and particularly worrying, to more frequent drinking of alcohol, particularly among young people who are most vulnerable to the promotion of alcohol in connection with sport and sports. The new regulation states that drinking alcohol with sport is inseparable and generally acceptable. Every day in Slovenia, for reasons exclusively related to alcohol, two people die and ten people are admitted to the hospital, including children. Let me specifically expose traffic damage. Alcoholised participants caused a thousand 855 accidents in 2016, of which 37 were fatal and 153 were serious injuries. The proportion of alcoholised accidents in recent years has increased. Economic damage is already high today and includes both health and social and material consequences. The assessment of the health and other costs associated with alcohol consumption in Slovenia was on average EUR 234 million per year between 2011 and 2014. With excise duties on alcoholic products, we do not yet cover the damage caused. While the Ministry of Health will devote more attention and money to preventive measures in this area in the coming years, the expected impact can be eliminated by further normalising the drinking of alcoholic beverages brought about by the amendment proposal. Otherwise, the bill is not appropriate. Some provisions of the bill are unclear, such as how to determine the amount of the fee for the sale of alcoholic beverages, and inappropriate, such as the provision that before the establishment of the register alcohol may be sold at sports events without permission. Although the main reason for the proposed changes is to increase the funding of sports organisations, the applicant did not give any approximate assessment of how much money will be collected and how and why it will be used. This fact is also recalled by the Legislative and Legal Service, which notes that the proposed solutions should be reconsidered and presented in a broader and more comprehensive manner their purpose and consequences. The proposed solutions will have financial implications for the state budget, the organisers and, indirectly, for the guests who are not fully and adequately represented in the draft law. For all these reasons, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia opposes the proposed legal changes. The competent commission of the Council of State has also confirmed this and is opposed by the professional public and non-governmental organisations. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u184	Thank you for your words, Mr President, but everyone has a very nice greeting. Part of the parliamentary group supports the bill and part two is against the proposal of the novel. Some of our Members also contributed to the signing with the desire that when alcohol can also be sold at sports events, it will help to improve the poor financial situation of many sports clubs. With this amendment, all organisers of public events should be authorised to sell alcoholic beverages, of course under certain conditions. Licences will be issued by administrative units upon payment of a fee for a period of 12 months. This would allow for a system of alcohol control at all public events, which should also increase security, as the provisions of the law offer the possibility of withdrawal in the event of serious infringements. At the same time, one of the statutory warnings for advertising alcoholic beverages will have to be published in a visible place on a poster or board, and there will also be increased control over the sale of drinks to minors, limited alcohol content in the liquor to be sold, and so on. The second part of our parliamentary group, however, joins the government's view that the proposal for a law is inappropriate for further consideration, as its possible adoption would increase the risk and harmful use of alcohol among the inhabitants of our country, as the number of places of sale would increase, which would increase the availability of alcohol. Some of our Members also agree that it is extremely inappropriate for sports and alcohol to be linked under the pretext of increasing the financing of sports companies by promoting the sale of alcoholic beverages at sports events. Such a release of access to alcohol will in no way help to achieve the goal that we have as a state set in accordance with public health guidelines, namely to reduce the number of over-indulgence alcohol drinkers from 10.2 % to 8 % by 2025. The opinion of the Government is also joined by the National Institute for Public Health, which considers that the proposed solution does not protect the health of the people of Slovenia and stresses that alcohol in Slovenia is a major public health and social problem. In 2015 the consumption of pure alcohol increased and exceeded the limit of five litres per year per person, which puts us firmly above the European average. In doing so, all opponents of the amendment also warn many NGOs that the costs of eliminating the consequences of alcohol consumption outweigh the benefits of the country for the sale of alcohol. Our vote on supporting the law will not be uniform in our parliamentary group. While part of the parliamentary group sees the bill as an effective means to improve the financial image of Slovenian sports clubs and societies, part two considers today an extremely sad day for public health in our country.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u203	Thank you, President. In principle, it is difficult to say anything negative about this marriage. In the normative framework, it brings something that we hope will also become a mental structure of criminal policy in Slovenia to start with softer forms of punishment, but it has one very big mistake, which is why we have also tabled this amendment. The transitional provisions provided for in Article 51 provide that, as from the date of the entry into force of this Act, the staff of CSDs engaged in tasks already in charge of the performance of the test tasks will be relocated to the newly established Probation Authority. There's one problem with that. This transitional provision states that these staff members will expire one day before the entry into force of the employment contract and will be employed on a new day on this board, which puts them in an inadequate position on the one hand. First of all, because they're going to be forced into a new administration without anyone asking them if they want to. Secondly, because they will not be able to pass on their advancements that they have already achieved in their jobs and because they will be able to enter the lowest appointment in this job by means of a new job. We find such a solution totally inappropriate, so we propose deleting this provision and propose that the Ministry prepare appropriate conditions for a call to be made, that those who want to do this work on a new board register this invitation and then go there. In this way, we will also preserve those employees who want to stay at CSD and do not empty the already existing human resources crisis at CSD and allow those who want to manage these tasks to move to this so-called new administration. By repeating, this solution seems totally unacceptable to us, so we are proposing a deletion and at the same time binding our support for the law on the adoption of this amendment, otherwise we will abstain from the law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u217	Thank you very much. It's not Friday, though. Honourable President of the National Assembly, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Protocol No 1 to the EEA Agreement should therefore be amended accordingly, 15 amending the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms is the result of the multi-annual efforts of the Member States of the Council of Europe to further reform the European Court of Human Rights, which has been facing backlogs for many years and to increase the effectiveness of the Court. I'll limit myself to showing some of the changes the protocol brings. First, it stresses the greater role and responsibility of national courts. It underlines the principle of subsidiarity, which means that the primary responsibility is to protect the rights and freedoms set out in the Convention of the State and their national courts, while this conduct remains under the supervision of the European Court of Human Rights, which remains the first to be responsible for the authoritarian interpretation of the Conventions and its Protocols. The second point is that the Protocol sets the maximum age limit for judges who may be proposed by the Member States to the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe for less than 65 years. The Protocol also removes the possibility for the parties to object to the decision of the Senate to refer the matter to the Grand Chamber if it assumes that the decision in the case may constitute a derogation from established case law. The aim of this measure is to contribute to the harmonisation of the case law of the Court. For individuals, it is important to amend the Protocol in order to shorten the period during which the parties can lodge a complaint with the European Court of Justice, from the current 6 to the current 4 months. Slovenia has actively participated and continues to participate in discussions on the reform of the ECHR, both at the professional level and at the political level. Slovenia supported the reform and also participated in the drafting of the protocol. Let me stress that the Protocol has already ratified 33 Member States of the Council of Europe, but that the Protocol is not optional and will only enter into force once it has been ratified by all countries, that is 47 States parties to the European Convention on Human Rights. If you, honourable Members, do so today, we will draw closer to this goal. Thank you very much.
