ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u219	Ladies and gentlemen of the House, ladies and gentlemen of the House! We have before us the proposal for a law on the ratification of the Minamata Convention on Living Silver. By means of the proposed law, the Republic of Slovenia shall take into its legal order the Convention, which was adopted after multi-annual coordination and preparation and for accession on 10 October 2013, when the Republic of Slovenia also acceded to it. By the end of April this year 128 countries had signed the Convention. It was currently ratified by 52 countries, and recently by the European Union and 7 Member States. The Convention will enter into force 90 days after the submission of the 50 instrument of ratification and then 90 days after the submission of the instrument of ratification for each country. Member States of the European Union shall endeavour to ratify the Convention so that as many as possible they can participate as full members at the first Conference of the Parties, which will be held in September this year. The Convention is a comprehensive international legal framework for the protection of human health and the environment against mercury. It introduces control over the acquisition, trade and use of mercury and its compounds, as well as a set of other measures to address its harmful effects. States shall encourage activities to reduce the release of mercury into the air, water and soil, and the rehabilitation of contaminated areas. It further promotes the mobilisation of the health sector, awareness-raising and education, and establishes mechanisms for research, international cooperation, information exchange and assistance. The proposed law before you today lays down, in accordance with these provisions, in addition to the official and inter-sectorally harmonised Slovenian text of the Convention, the competent authorities for its implementation at the national level. These tasks will be carried out by the Office of the Republic of Slovenia for Chemicals at the Ministry of Health and the Ministry of Environment and Space. This definition of competence reflects and complements their already existing tasks in the field of health and environment protection and the powers to implement the already established legislation on mercury.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u220	A little peace in the hall, please!
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-24-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u221	In the exercise of these powers, they will cooperate with Slovenian institutions already operating in areas governed by the Convention, such as the Institute of Joseph Stefan and the Center for the Management of the Heritage of Living Silver Idrija. As a result of the long-standing acquisition of mercury in Idrija, the Republic of Slovenia has actively participated in the process of establishing a globally binding agreement in order to monitor, manage and remedy the consequences of this activity. The Convention sets out a provision on cooperation in the areas of capacity-building and technical assistance between existing national, regional and sub-regional centres also opens wide opportunities for international cooperation and sharing of knowledge, experience and programmes implemented in Slovenia by the Centre for the Management of the Heritage of Living Silver Idrija, as well as other established research institutes, in particular the Institute of Joseph Stefan. In the European Union, the responsibility for implementing the Convention is shared between the EU and the Member States. Much of the provisions of the Convention are subject to harmonised legislation and are already being adopted in the European Union by a number of chemical and environmental regulations. In order to ratify the Convention in the Member States, some other areas falling within the competence of the European Community had to be regulated in advance. In 2015 and 2016, an intensive preparation and coordination of the European mercury regulation was carried out in the European Union, which is now concluded. By this Regulation, Member States have adopted certain common implementing rules for the implementation of the Convention in their legal order, while at the national level regulating areas falling within national competence. Only on the basis of such a coordinated process of the Convention can the Member States ratify it. Thank you very much.

It is precisely because of the direct link with the acquisition of mercury in Idrija by monitoring, controlling and remedying the consequences that the general concern and interest share that the issue of mercury is also being addressed at the global level. Therefore, the adoption of the Law on the ratification of the Minamata Convention on Living Silver is a key act. The implementation of the Convention is intertwined with the common policy of the European Union and the implementation of its legislation and the implementation of the strategy for joint action in the field of mercury. In Idrija and Slovenia, a number of specific knowledge has been developed and maintained concerning all areas of production, processing, use and ecological and health issues of mercury. In these areas, Slovenia is one of the world's leaders. It is because of these facts that the State Public Institute for the Management of the Heritage of Living Silver Idrija, which has already taken over the activities of maintaining the indemnified part of the cave and monitoring of the influential area of the Idrija mine, has also included in its statutes and strategic guidelines cooperation in the implementation of the Minamat Convention on Living Silver in agreement with the competent authorities in the Republic of Slovenia and with related centres at international level and with the UNEP Secretariat. In accordance with its long-term strategic orientations, the Centre may, together with the competent authorities in the Republic of Slovenia and other institutions, pay particular attention to the implementation of the provisions of Articles 12, 16, 17, 18 and 19 of the Convention, thereby establishing a functioning with the secretariat of the United Nations Environment Programme of UNEP and other related organisations at international level. This would enable the exchange of information on scientific research results and establish active cooperation at a global network organisation dealing with mercury issues – UNEP. The establishment of cooperation with the secretariats of the chemicals and waste conventions may also be welcome. The preparation of such projects would, inter alia, give the Centre the possibility of drawing financial resources from the Global Environment Facility (GEF). Thank you.
